Was there any major stress or mood change around the time they began?\n\nPatient: I've actually struggled with organization and forgetfulness since I was a kid, definitely before I turned 12, but it has become a major problem over the last 11 months. I just know that I struggle with organizing my tasks and forgetting things in general, no matter the situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble getting started on tasks, estimating how long things will take, or putting things off until the last minute even when they’re important?\n\nPatient: Yes, that describes my struggles perfectly. I often put things off because I just don't know how to get started on organizing them. I also constantly misjudge how much time a task will actually take me, which makes planning my day really difficult."

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I've been struggling a lot lately with organizing my daily tasks, and it's starting to really overwhelm me. I keep forgetting things I need to do, and I just can't seem to keep my life in order.\n\nDoctor: Have these problems with organization and forgetfulness been present since childhood, or did they start more recently? Was there any major stress or mood change around the time they began?\n\nPatient: I've actually struggled with organization and forgetfulness since I was a kid, definitely before I turned 12, but it has become a major problem over the last 11 months. I don't recall having any major stress or mood changes when these issues first started back then.\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and organization problems affect you in more than one area of life, like both work or school and home, and did they also cause problems in those settings when you were younger?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely affect me in both areas of my life. It was the same way when I was a kid, too, causing issues both at home and in school.\n\nDoctor: Over the past several months, have you also been feeling down or losing interest in things, or spending a lot of time feeling worried and unable to control the worrying?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really been feeling down or losing interest in things. I also don't think I've had any uncontrollable worry, it's just the frustration of trying to keep up with everything.\n\nDoctor: Besides organization and forgetfulness, do you often make careless mistakes, have trouble staying focused on tasks or conversations, avoid tasks that need a lot of mental effort, or lose things you need?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those other issues like making careless mistakes or losing my things. My main struggles are definitely just the organization and forgetfulness that I mentioned earlier.\n\nDoctor: Do you often get easily distracted by things going on around you, or have trouble following through and finishing tasks once you start them?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I get easily distracted by my surroundings. And once I actually manage to get started on a task, I don't really have trouble finishing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble listening when someone is speaking directly to you, or find yourself misplacing everyday items like keys, phone, or paperwork?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have trouble listening when someone is speaking directly to me. I also don't typically misplace everyday items like my keys or my phone.\n\nDoctor: When you forget tasks or have trouble organizing, is it mainly because you have difficulty planning and prioritizing even when you're calm, or does it mostly happen when you're overwhelmed by stress or too many responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure, and I haven't really thought about whether it happens more when I'm calm or stressed. I just know that I struggle with organizing my tasks and forgetting things in general, no matter the situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble getting started on tasks, estimating how long things will take, or putting things off until the last minute even when they’re important?\n\nPatient: Yes, that describes my struggles perfectly. I often put things off because I just don't know how to get started on organizing them. I also constantly misjudge how much time a task will actually take me, which makes planning my day really difficult.\n\nDoctor: When you were a child, did teachers or family notice that you were often forgetful in daily activities or had trouble organizing schoolwork and assignments?"
